=== Throttle HUD [spacelab.throttle-hud/usage]
A per-seat throttle lever that any game can place in its HUD through the hud.throttle service.


## What it does
This module draws the vertical THR lever beside each local player's view. A player drags it, or focuses it and uses the arrow, Page and Home/End keys, to set engine thrust from 0 to 100%. It is an instrument: a small piece of HUD that a HUD owner creates once per seat (one local player) and viewport, then updates every rendered frame. The module reads flight data and sends throttle requests only through an adapter that the game supplies, so it never touches the world, physics or saves.

## Provides and requires
- Provides hud.throttle@1.0.0 through the THROTTLE_HUD key: a HudInstrumentDefinition with ID spacelab.throttle-hud.
- The module itself requires no services. Each lever it creates needs the view.flight-hud-state@1.0.0 adapter (FLIGHT_HUD_STATE), which the game passes to the HUD owner for every seat. A HUD without that adapter skips the lever instead of failing.
- The HUD owner is ui.view-hud@1.0.0 (VIEW_HUD, from spacelab.view-hud). Space Sim and Flight starter select THROTTLE_HUD into every seat's view HUD.

## Settings
- keyboardStepPercent (default 1, from 0.1 to 100, step 0.1): percentage points added or removed by one Up or Down arrow press on a focused lever.
- pageStepPercent (default 10, from 0.1 to 100, step 0.1): percentage points added or removed by one Page Up or Page Down press. Home always selects 0% and End 100%.
- Both settings only change keyboard steps. Session ownership and input locks still decide whether a request is applied.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization provides one shared, stateless instrument definition and this guide. The module owns no resources of its own.
- create runs once per seat. It builds the lever element and its listeners; the first update attaches it to the HUD surface root.
- rebind (a new target, player or session) and cancel stop any drag in progress. A drag also stops when the target ship changes or the adapter reports the lever as locked.
- update runs every frame with wall-clock seconds. It reads the adapter's throttle state and positions the lever; the game may move it with placeThrottle, for example to follow a saved HUD layout. draw adds no overlay shapes.
- dispose removes the element and its window listener. The HUD owner disposes a lever when its seat is removed, when the instrument is deselected, or when one of its methods throws.

## Remove or replace
Space Sim and Flight starter list hud.throttle as a required slot, so their targets need a provider. Players hide the levers with Settings → HUD visibility → Throttle levers, which keeps the module loaded. Physics, saves, the navball, cameras and split screen do not depend on the lever.
To replace it, write a presentation module that lists hud.throttle in slots and provides hud.throttle@1.0.0 with its own HudInstrumentDefinition, then select it in the game profile with "providers": { "hud.throttle": "your.module-id" }. Send thrust only through FlightHudAdapter.setThrottle so that ownership checks still apply.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: the HUD contracts can still change before the API is frozen, and a breaking change will come with a migration note. The legacy presentation ID builtin.throttle is a compatibility record that keeps the on/off choice in older presentation profiles; new code uses THROTTLE_HUD.

## Example
examples/modules/throttle-hud is a minimal replacement provider: a text readout with − and + buttons that fills hud.throttle@1.0.0. It shows the full instrument lifecycle and reads its step from a setting. Build and check it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/throttle-hud/module.json /tmp/throttle-readout.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/throttle-readout.spacemod

=== Bounded orbital transfer search [spacelab.transfer-planner/usage]
Editable bounded same-body and interbody transfers over selected moving-frame prediction contracts.


## What it does
This module provides a generator-based bounded solver evaluating circular, elliptical, same-body, and interbody transfer trajectories and intermediate parking orbits over verified prediction contracts. It compares departure apsides, solves tangential velocity increments, checks terrain contact clearances, and structures candidate routes into efficient and faster options.

## Provides and requires
- Provides flight.transfers@1.0.0 through the TRANSFERS service key: creates TransferPlanner generator instances.
- Fills the flight.transfers slot in presentation profiles.
- Requires flight.plans@^1.0.0: integrates with flight plan node creation and route evaluation.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization resolves the flight plan service and provides TRANSFERS.
- withBurns(factory) optionally binds a finite maneuver burn factory to refine burn durations and capture validation.
- search(world, request) returns a generator yielding intermediate TransferProgress objects and returning a final TransferResult.
- Each generator step performs bounded cooperative search calculations without advancing live simulation time.
- Search cancellation terminates child contexts and releases allocated prediction seeds.

## Remove or replace
To customize orbital transfer trajectory search algorithms or add specialized planetary encounter heuristics, contribute an alternative provider for flight.transfers@1.0.0 to the flight.transfers slot. Removing this module disables transfer window searches while preserving manual node editing.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: search iteration limits and route option data structures may evolve prior to final API freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/transfer-planner demonstrates querying transfer solver capabilities and stepping through transfer search progress. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/transfer-planner/module.json /tmp/example-transfer-planner.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-transfer-planner.spacemod

=== Reusable tutorial runner [spacelab.tutorial-runner/usage]
Read-only objectives, versioned local progress and explicit knowledge checks.


## What it does
Provides a bounded, read-only tutorial state machine that evaluates scalar flight observations and multiple-choice knowledge checks without touching simulation state, DOM elements, timers or session storage directly. Each lesson step requires either simulation-checked conditions or an explicit knowledge question. Timed holds accrue only across consecutive unpaused simulation seconds, and any change in flight continuity identity or simulation time rewind interrupts the active attempt.

## Provides and requires
- Provides learning.runner@1.0.0 through the TUTORIAL_RUNNERS service key: creates independent TutorialRunner instances.
- Fills the learning.runner slot in presentation and platform profiles.
- Requires nothing. Consumers such as spacelab.academy-ui declare learning.runner@^1.0.0; if no provider fills learning.runner, dependent modules fail profile resolution.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization registers TUTORIAL_RUNNERS and contributes this guide.
- create({ storage, storageKey }) loads up to 256 completed lesson strings (each at most 160 characters) from storageKey; unavailable or corrupt storage falls back silently to an in-memory Set.
- start(lesson, observation) validates that lesson has 1 to 64 steps, each step has either 1 to 32 conditions or a valid question (never both), holdSeconds is finite and non-negative, and allowPaused steps have holdSeconds === 0; it then records observation.values as the baseline and observation.identity as the continuity token.
- update(observation) interrupts the attempt if observation.identity changed, observation.time is non-finite or observation.time decreased; otherwise it checks every condition against current and baseline values and advances once held for holdSeconds consecutive unpaused simulation seconds (or immediately when allowPaused is true).
- answer(choice) checks the 0-based choice index against the current step's question, sets explanation feedback and advances on a correct answer; when the final step completes, <lesson.id>@<lesson.version> is added to completed and persisted.
- stop() clears the active lesson and hold state; dispose() stops the runner and causes subsequent start() calls to throw.

## Remove or replace
Provide another learning.runner@1.0.0 implementation in the learning.runner slot to customize condition evaluation or progress persistence. Removing this module is supported when no enabled module requires learning.runner; if spacelab.academy-ui is enabled, removing learning.runner prevents profile resolution.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: condition operators and runner diagnostics may be refined before final freeze. Completion entries use the versioned <lesson.id>@<lesson.version> format.

## Example
examples/modules/tutorial-runner creates a runner, evaluates a timed scalar hold objective and answers a knowledge check through learning.runner. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/tutorial-runner/module.json /tmp/example-tutorial-runner.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-tutorial-runner.spacemod

## Related
-> Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation [modules]
-> API reference: Tutorial runner [api-reference/module/spacelab.tutorial-runner]
-> game-api: tutorials.ts [api-reference/game-api/tutorials]

=== Window shell and dock [spacelab.ui-shell/usage]
Optional grouped dock and window lifecycle.


## What it does
Provides a window shell: grouped panels, a dock of buttons, optional instrument windows, modal Escape handling, fullscreen-Escape handling and import-to-panel routing, all on top of shared windows. It creates no game-specific panels; the host supplies panel definitions, borrowed content nodes and callbacks. The game can omit the shell entirely, and nothing here touches gameplay or session authority.

## Provides and requires
- Provides ui.shell@1.0.0 through the WINDOW_SHELL service key: creates window shells for a viewport.
- Fills the ui.shell slot in platform profiles.
- Requires ui.windows@^1.0.0 through the WINDOWS service key: supplies window chrome and icons.
- Also exports createActionDock for grouped game-specific actions that share native vector button chrome.

## Settings
- escapeToggleMs (default 300, from 0 to 1000, step 1): minimum wall-time interval in milliseconds between Escape or fullscreen toggles. Raising it ignores rapid repeats; 0 allows every press.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization captures WINDOWS, registers WINDOW_SHELL and contributes this guide.
- create(options) builds one shell for a viewport from grouped panel specs and borrowed content nodes. The shell owns its windows, dock entries, subscriptions, keyboard and file listeners and styles.
- addInstrument(window, label, hint) adds an optional dock entry for a borrowed window and returns a lease; dispose the lease when the instrument is removed. The shell does not own borrowed instrument windows.
- open, close, toggle and handleFullscreenEscape drive the panels; release and focus are host callbacks for input.
- Disposing a shell, or the provider, removes listeners and styles and restores borrowed nodes to their original locations. A dock lease restores the controls it borrowed.

## Remove or replace
Provide another ui.shell@1.0.0 implementation in the ui.shell slot to change how panels and the dock look and behave. Removing the module leaves the game without a grouped dock; any host code that requires ui.shell must handle that, while gameplay and sessions are unaffected.

## Stability
Module version 0.2.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: details may change before the final freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/ui-shell wraps one panel in a shell through the public ui.shell service, showing the factory contract and borrowed-content lifecycle. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/ui-shell/module.json /tmp/example-ui-shell.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-ui-shell.spacemod

## Related
-> Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation [modules]

-> game-api: window-shell.ts [api-reference/game-api/window-shell]

=== Shared windows [spacelab.ui-windows/usage]
Independent movable, resizable and disposable windows through public ui.windows@1.


## What it does
Provides shared floating windows and structured window content. Windows give independent chrome (moving, resizing, pin, minimize, maximize, stacking) around host-supplied containers. Window content mounts documents, instructions, inspectors, forms, lists, logs and versioned previews as plain text and DOM. The module owns layout chrome only; layout preferences never carry authority, and titles and all content strings are rendered as text.

## Provides and requires
- Provides ui.windows@1.0.0 through the WINDOWS service key: creates windows and one shared window system per factory.
- Provides ui.window-content@1.0.0 through the WINDOW_CONTENT service key: mounts structured content and registers preview types.
- Fills the ui.windows and ui.window-content slots in platform profiles.
- Requires nothing.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization registers each service only when selected for its slot and contributes this guide.
- WINDOWS.create(options) returns one window for a container, ID and title, with optional viewer preference storage. Windows created by one system share a stack; each provider has an independent stack, so borrowed chat window interactions use the same WindowSystem.
- WINDOW_CONTENT.mount and document mount content into a root and return a lease. register({type:{name,version},validate,mount}) publishes an exact-version preview type; its mounts own their camera and renderer, receive immutable bounded data and must dispose every read-only subscription. Missing versions fail preparation; unregistering closes all instances of that type.
- Dispose window and content leases on removal. Disposing the provider closes remaining windows, releases styles and stops retained callbacks. A game supplies its own dismiss gesture, theme decoration and dock.

## Remove or replace
Provide another ui.windows@1.0.0 or ui.window-content@1.0.0 implementation in the matching slot to restyle or replace chrome or content rendering. Removing this module removes shared windows from every module that requires them, including the shell and the Manual.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: details may change before the final freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/ui-windows opens a movable notice window through the public ui.windows service and disposes it. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/ui-windows/module.json /tmp/example-ui-windows.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-ui-windows.spacemod

=== Universe file storage [spacelab.universe-files/usage]
Bounded browser files and owned downloads, independent of save validation.


## What it does
This module provides bounded browser file reading and download triggering for universe save archives. It reads user-selected Blobs up to 64 MiB into byte arrays and dispatches downloads by creating temporary object URLs for generated archives. It tracks object URLs and revokes them upon a 10-second timeout or immediate provider disposal. It performs no decoding, world inspection, package validation, or engine state activation.

## Provides and requires
- Provides universe.files@1.0.0 through the UNIVERSE_FILES service key: creates UniverseFiles file reading and download services.
- Fills the universe.files slot in platform profiles.
- Requires no additional services.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization registers the UNIVERSE_FILES service and contributes this guide.
- read(file, signal) validates that the Blob size does not exceed 64 MiB, checks for cancellation, and resolves array buffer bytes.
- write(name, bytes, signal) validates filename length and characters, creates an object URL, clicks a temporary anchor tag to start the download, and schedules automatic URL revocation after 10 seconds.
- dispose clears pending revocation timers, revokes all tracked object URLs, and causes subsequent operations to throw.

## Remove or replace
To customize file transfer or route archives to cloud or IndexedDB storage, contribute an alternative universe.files@1.0.0 provider to the universe.files slot. Removing this module leaves browser save interfaces without file input reading or download triggering.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: object URL timeouts and filename validation patterns may evolve before final freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/universe-files demonstrates reading and writing universe archive bytes through the UNIVERSE_FILES provider. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/universe-files/module.json /tmp/example-universe-files.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-universe-files.spacemod

## Related
-> Code modules: manifests, packaging and installation [modules]
-> API reference: Universe file storage [api-reference/module/spacelab.universe-files]

=== Universe save controls [spacelab.universe-save-controls/usage]
Independent Save universe / Load universe UI over explicit commands and file storage.


## What it does
This module provides mounting for Save universe and Load universe buttons and a file selection picker into a DOM container element. It connects user click and file selection events to caller-owned universe save commands and browser file reader and download services. It tracks busy state to disallow concurrent operations, invokes caller callbacks on success or failure, and cleanly restores original DOM children upon disposal.

## Provides and requires
- Provides ui.universe-saves@1.0.0 through the UNIVERSE_SAVE_CONTROLS service key: mounts Save and Load universe UI controls.
- Fills the ui.universe-saves slot in platform profiles.
- Requires universe.files@^1.0.0 through the UNIVERSE_FILES service key: performs browser file reading and download triggering.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization captures UNIVERSE_FILES, registers UNIVERSE_SAVE_CONTROLS, and contributes this guide.
- mount(options) borrows caller-provided buttons or creates default Save universe and Load universe DOM buttons and a hidden file input inside options.root.
- Save button click triggers commands.save using files.write, notifying options.saved upon successful completion.
- Load button click triggers file input picker, and file selection invokes commands.load using files.read, notifying options.loaded.
- dispose detaches event listeners, aborts in-flight control operations, restores original DOM children, and clears control references.

## Remove or replace
To customize save dialog styling or replace default buttons with a custom menu widget, provide an alternative ui.universe-saves@1.0.0 implementation in the ui.universe-saves slot. Removing this module omits the default Save and Load DOM controls from pause menus and navigation shells.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: button identifiers, prefixes, and event handling semantics may evolve before final freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/universe-save-controls demonstrates mounting Save and Load controls and handling operation callbacks. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/universe-save-controls/module.json /tmp/example-universe-save-controls.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-universe-save-controls.spacemod

=== Universe save commands [spacelab.universe-saves/usage]
Cancellable save/load over engine validation and independently selected storage.


## What it does
This module provides single-operation save and load command controllers for universe sessions. It coordinates world state serialization and container encoding while restricting each controller to at most one in-flight operation. It verifies session and world identity guards prior to publication, ensuring that session transitions occurring during asynchronous operations abort safely without corrupting active worlds. The host retains package recovery, container decoding safety, and engine activation.

## Provides and requires
- Provides universe.saves@1.0.0 through the UNIVERSE_SAVES service key: creates UniverseSaveCommands controllers.
- Fills the universe.saves slot in platform profiles.
- Requires core.save@^1.0.0 through the CORE_SAVE service key: serializes active simulation state into WorldSave records.
- Requires core.universe-save-format@^1.0.0 through the CORE_UNIVERSE_FORMAT service key: encodes and decodes container archive bytes.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization captures CORE_SAVE and CORE_UNIVERSE_FORMAT, registers UNIVERSE_SAVES, and contributes this guide.
- create(options) instantiates a UniverseSaveCommands controller tracking single-operation execution and active session identity.
- save(write, signal) captures session snapshot identity, invokes core.save, encodes container bytes, verifies identity guard, and writes formatted .spaceuniverse files.
- load(read, signal) invokes read, enforces the 64 MiB limit, decodes container data via format.decode, checks identity guard, and restores the world.
- dispose cancels pending save or load tasks and disassociates active controllers.

## Remove or replace
To customize save orchestration, permissions checking, or session migration rules, provide an alternative universe.saves@1.0.0 implementation in the universe.saves slot. Removing this module leaves universe save and load workflows without coordinated session guards and format delegation.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: guard checks and error reporting may be adjusted before final freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/universe-saves demonstrates creating save commands, executing serialized saves, and verifying session guards. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/universe-saves/module.json /tmp/example-universe-saves.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-universe-saves.spacemod

=== Server universe storage [spacelab.universe-server-storage/usage]
Serialized atomic saves, backups and validated recovery over borrowed native disk IO.


## What it does
This module provides persistent server-side universe storage for Node environments. It manages serialized asynchronous disk writes via temporary file writes and atomic replacement, maintains timestamped historical backups, quarantines corrupted save files, and attempts automatic recovery from prior backups. It distinguishes engine and package configuration errors from save corruption, ensuring missing packages do not result in quarantined data.

## Provides and requires
- Provides universe.server-storage@1.0.0 through the SERVER_UNIVERSE_STORAGE service key: creates ServerUniverseStore storage instances.
- Fills the universe.server-storage slot in platform profiles.
- Requires core.universe-disk@^1.0.0 through the CORE_UNIVERSE_DISK service key: performs low-level native filesystem IO operations.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization captures CORE_UNIVERSE_DISK, registers SERVER_UNIVERSE_STORAGE, and contributes this guide.
- create(options) instantiates a ServerUniverseStore instance bound to options.directory and backup retention settings.
- save(save) enqueues serialized world data writes, writing to a temporary file and atomically renaming it to world.json.
- load() reads world.json, invokes options.restore, falls back to timestamped backups if corrupt, and rethrows configuration errors without quarantine.
- backup() copies the current active world file into a timestamped backup and trims historical backups beyond keepBackups.
- idle() returns a Promise resolving when all currently queued disk writes have completed.
- dispose marks the store closed, rejecting subsequent writes and unregistering active references.

## Remove or replace
To customize server universe persistence (such as backing by a relational database or external object store), provide an alternative universe.server-storage@1.0.0 implementation in the universe.server-storage slot. Removing this module omits automated disk persistence and backup rotation in dedicated Node servers.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: backup file naming formats and quarantine conventions may be refined before final freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/universe-server-storage demonstrates creating a server universe store, saving world state, and managing backups. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/universe-server-storage/module.json /tmp/example-universe-server-storage.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-universe-server-storage.spacemod

## Related

-> API reference: Server universe storage [api-reference/module/spacelab.universe-server-storage]
-> game-api: universe-saving.ts [api-reference/game-api/universe-saving]

=== Shared view HUD lifecycle [spacelab.view-hud/usage]
Optional instruments with independent per-view lifetimes.


## What it does
This module provides a robust multi-instrument HUD manager with independent lifetimes per seat and viewport. It coordinates resizing, input gesture cancellations, updates, and overlay drawing across contributed flight instruments while isolating instrument faults so an error in one instrument never disrupts others or crashes the host view.

## Provides and requires
- Provides ui.view-hud@1.0.0 through the VIEW_HUD service key: creates ViewHud managers for viewport HUD surfaces.
- Fills the ui.view-hud slot in platform configurations.
- Requires no external services.

## Settings
This module has no settings.

## Lifecycle
- Initialization contributes this guide and registers the VIEW_HUD factory.
- create(options) instantiates a ViewHud controller bound to a viewport and seat identity.
- select(definitions) dynamically activates declared instruments matching available service adapters and tears down unselected instruments.
- resize(surface) adjusts CSS-pixel dimensions and dispatches new surface bounds to active instruments.
- update(elapsed) steps instrument logic with wall-clock elapsed seconds.
- draw(into) collects vector overlay primitives; shapes appended before a fault are discarded.
- dispose terminates all active instruments cleanly.

## Remove or replace
To customize HUD instrument hosting, implement ui.view-hud@1.0.0 in the ui.view-hud slot. Removing this module disables instrument aggregation and overlays across flight viewports.

## Stability
Module version 0.1.0 uses API space-sim-game-api/2 and manifest format space-sim-module/2. Both are pre-freeze: instrument definition schemas and fault notification interfaces may evolve before API freeze.

## Example
examples/modules/view-hud demonstrates creating a ViewHud instance, selecting instrument definitions, and drawing overlays. Build and validate it from the repository root:
    npm run module:build -- examples/modules/view-hud/module.json /tmp/example-view-hud.spacemod
    npm run module:validate -- /tmp/example-view-hud.spacemod
